- **lens-2-anatomy**: Lens 2 of the Doctoral Problem Statement Lenses. Use when a doctoral student pastes a draft problem statement and asks to "run the anatomy," "check the four functions," "does my problem statement do everything it needs to," or "map my draft against the required parts." Maps the text against the four functions a problem statement must perform, quoting the exact words or marking ABSENT. Never supplies missing content. ([RHuebner1972/doctoral-second-brain](https://github.com/RHuebner1972/doctoral-second-brain/tree/HEAD/skills/problem-statement-lens-2-anatomy) · ★2)
- **lens-3-evidence-audit**: Lens 3 of the Doctoral Problem Statement Lenses. Use when a doctoral student pastes a draft problem statement and asks to "run the evidence audit," "which claims are cited," "what am I asserting without support," or "audit the evidence in my problem statement." Classifies every factual claim as CITED, UNCITED, or UNFALSIFIABLE and names the kind of evidence each would need. Never suggests or names sources. ([RHuebner1972/doctoral-second-brain](https://github.com/RHuebner1972/doctoral-second-brain/tree/HEAD/skills/problem-statement-lens-3-evidence-audit) · ★2)
- **lens-4-symptom-or-root-cause**: Lens 4 of the Doctoral Problem Statement Lenses. Use when a doctoral student pastes a draft problem statement and asks "is this the real problem or a symptom," "run the root cause lens," "what might be underneath my problem," or "symptom or root cause." Treats the stated problem as a possible symptom and proposes three plausible underlying conditions with what would need to be observed to rule each in or out. Never says which is correct. ([RHuebner1972/doctoral-second-brain](https://github.com/RHuebner1972/doctoral-second-brain/tree/HEAD/skills/problem-statement-lens-4-symptom-or-root-cause) · ★2)
- **lens-5-assumption-excavation**: Lens 5 of the Doctoral Problem Statement Lenses. Use when a doctoral student pastes a draft problem statement and asks to "excavate assumptions," "what am I assuming," "run the assumption lens," or "what has to be true for this to hold." Lists every assumption the statement depends on, split into stated and unstated, and for each unstated one writes the sentence the author would have to defend before a committee. Does not evaluate or rewrite. ([RHuebner1972/doctoral-second-brain](https://github.com/RHuebner1972/doctoral-second-brain/tree/HEAD/skills/problem-statement-lens-5-assumption-excavation) · ★2)
- **lens-6-adversarial-committee**: Lens 6 of the Doctoral Problem Statement Lenses. Use when a doctoral student pastes a draft problem statement and asks for "the adversarial committee," "attack my problem statement," "hardest defense questions," "play the hostile committee member," or "run lens 6." Role-plays a skeptical committee member voting against the proposal and produces the three hardest questions, the strongest argument that it is not research-worthy, and the first sentence to attack. Run LAST, never first; if earlier lenses have not been run, say so before proceeding. ([RHuebner1972/doctoral-second-brain](https://github.com/RHuebner1972/doctoral-second-brain/tree/HEAD/skills/problem-statement-lens-6-adversarial-committee) · ★2)
